\nu
, by Remark
12.13
. As a result, the integral

|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}F(\ell)V_{g,Q}^{\nu,\mathfrak{T},j}(\ell)\,\mathrm{d}\ell|
is bounded above by

2^{(n_{\mathbf{S}}+2j)(Q+n_{\mathbf{S}})}e^{12R\chi(\mathbf{S})}\lVert F(\ell)\,e^{(1+\eta)\ell}\rVert_{\infty}g^{-\chi(\mathbf{S})}
times

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{\alpha},\mathbf{x}>0\\
Y\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{S}|\mathbf{S}_{\partial})\end{subarray}}e^{-\eta\ell_{Y}({\mathbf{\zeta}})}\prod_{i=1}^{j}e^{\alpha_{i}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{[0,\kappa]}(\alpha_{i})|\nu(\tau_{(\mathbf{x},Y)},\vec{\alpha})|\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Vol}^{\mathrm{WP}}_{g_{\mathbf{S}},n_{\mathbf{S}}}(\mathbf{x},Y)\,\mathrm{d}\vec{\alpha}.

We now integrate
\vec{\alpha}
on
[0,\kappa]^{j}
, using the upper bound (
12.12
),
to bound the previous integral by

\displaystyle(e^{\kappa}-1)^{j}\mathfrak{f}(j,\chi(\tau))\int_{\mathbf{x},Y\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{S}|\mathbf{S}_{\partial})}e^{-\eta\ell_{Y}({\mathbf{\zeta}})}\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Vol}^{\mathrm{WP}}_{g_{\mathbf{S}},n_{\mathbf{S}}}(\mathbf{x},Y)

where the integral runs over the set of
\mathbf{x}>0
and
Y\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{S}|\mathbf{S}_{\partial})
. We
conclude by noticing that the integral above is bounded by

\mathrm{fn}(\chi(\mathbf{S}),\eta)e^{9\eta R\chi(\tau)}
. Indeed, we showed in
[
3
]
that, because

\tau
is a derived tangle, we can construct a multi-loop
\gamma_{\tau}
, filling
\tau
, of total
length
\leq 9R\chi(\tau)
. We then conclude using an adaptation of Corollary
3.25
,
proving that the Weil–Petersson volume of the set

\{(\mathbf{x},Y),\mathbf{x}>0,Y\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{S}|\mathbf{S}_{\partial}),\ell_{Y}({\mathbf{\zeta}})+\ell_{Y}(\gamma_{\tau})\leq\ell\}

grows at most polynomially in
\ell
, because

(\mathbf{\zeta},\gamma_{\tau})
fills
\mathbf{S}
.
∎

12.5.3.
Preliminary step

We are now ready to detail the proof of Theorem
12.16
, which we do in the next few
paragraphs. First, Lemma
A.2
shows that we can restrict attention to realizations
of rank
r_{g}^{\rho_{j}\mathbf{S}}(\mathfrak{R})\leq N
in the sum (
12.10
), modulo an error

\leq\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,\chi(\mathbf{S}))\frac{2^{2j(Q+n_{\mathbf{S}})}}{g^{N+1}}\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t\in\tilde{\partial}\mathbf{S}}z_{t}\Big).

Given
Q
,
N
and
\mathbf{S}
, the number of such realizations is bounded: we are reduced
to dealing with a finite number of terms in (
12.10
). We therefore pick a realisation

\mathfrak{R}\in R_{g,Q}(\rho_{j}\mathbf{S})
.

12.5.4.
First step

We now use the result of
[
1
]
(recalled in the appendix as Theorem

A.1
) to write an approximate expression (up to errors decaying like

1/g^{N+1}
) of each term

\frac{1}{V_{g}}\prod_{t\in\tilde{\partial}\mathbf{S}}z_{t}V_{\mathfrak{R}}(\mathbf{z})

in the definition of
\Phi_{g,Q}^{\mathfrak{T},j}(\mathbf{z})
, see equation (
12.10
). More precisely, the quantity
above is well-approximated by a function
F_{g,\mathfrak{R}}^{(N)}(\mathbf{z})
of the form

(12.13)

\displaystyle F_{g,\mathfrak{R}}^{(N)}(\mathbf{z}):=\sum_{V_{+},V_{-},V_{0}}P_{g,\mathfrak{R}}^{(N,V_{\pm})}(\mathbf{z})\prod_{t\in V_{+}}\cosh\left(\frac{z_{t}}{2}\right)\prod_{t\in V_{-}}\sinh\left(\frac{z_{t}}{2}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi_{i}^{l}=0\\
J_{l}^{i}=\{t,t^{\prime}\}\end{subarray}}z_{t}\delta(z_{t}-z_{t^{\prime}})

where:

•

V_{+},V_{-},V_{0}
runs over partitions of the set

V:=\tilde{\partial}\mathbf{S}\setminus J^{\mathrm{cyl}}
;

•

the function
P_{g,\mathfrak{R}}^{(N,V_{\pm})}
is a polynomial function in the variables

(z_{t})_{t\in V}
, of total degree in
(z_{t})_{t\in V_{+}\sqcup V_{-}}
is
\leq 2N
and partial
degree with respect to each
(z_{t})_{t\in V_{0}}
less than
a_{N+1}
.

The error made in this approximation can be bounded above by

\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,\chi(\mathbf{S}))\,\tilde{a}_{N}^{2j}\frac{(\|\mathbf{z}\|+1)^{3N+1}}{g^{N+1}}\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t\in\tilde{\partial}\mathbf{S}}z_{t}\Big).

By Theorem

A.1
and (
A.3
), the coefficients of the polynomials

P_{g,\mathfrak{R}}^{(N,V_{\pm})}
are bounded by

\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,\chi(\mathbf{S}))\tilde{a}_{N}^{n_{\mathbf{S}}+2j}\frac{V_{g_{\mathbf{S}},n_{\mathbf{S}}}}{V_{g}}V_{\mathfrak{R}}(\mathbf{0})\leq\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,\chi(\mathbf{S}))\frac{\tilde{a}_{N}^{2j}}{g^{\chi(\mathbf{S})}}.

12.5.5.
Second step: the Friedman–Ramanujan property

The leading term in Theorem
12.16
is obtained by replacing the function

\Phi_{g,Q}^{\mathfrak{T},j}(\mathbf{z})
by its approximate expression up to errors
1/g^{N+1}
. It is thus of
the form (
12.9
), with

\phi(\mathbf{x},\vec{\alpha})=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{j}}\tilde{\Phi}_{g,Q}^{\mathfrak{T},j}(\mathbf{x},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{j},\alpha_{j}),

where
\tilde{\Phi}_{g,Q}^{\mathfrak{T},j}(\mathbf{z})
is a sum of finitely many terms of the form
(
12.13
). We are left with the question of proving that each function of
\ell
defined by

(12.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\mathbf{x}>0\end{subarray}}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{S}|\mathbf{S}_{\partial})\\
\ell_{Y}({\mathbf{\zeta}})=\ell\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{j}}F_{g,\mathfrak{R}}^{(N)}(\mathbf{x},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{j},\alpha_{j})\,\nu(\tau_{(\mathbf{x},Y)},\vec{\alpha})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{\alpha}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Vol}^{\mathrm{WP}}_{g_{\mathbf{S}},n_{\mathbf{S}}}(\mathbf{x},Y)}{\,\mathrm{d}\ell}

is a Friedman–Ramanujan function, with estimates on its norm.

The proof of this fact is the same as the proof of Theorem
9.4
, applied to the
multi-loop
(\mathbf{\zeta},\partial\tau,c_{1},\ldots,c_{j})
, with the set
W
corresponding to loops in

\partial\tau\cup(c_{1},\ldots,c_{j})
,
\kappa_{i}:=\kappa
for loops in
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{j})
,
and
\sum_{i\in\partial\tau}\kappa_{i}\leq 12R\chi(\mathbf{S})
.

The set of
a priori neutral
variables is enlarged to include the parameters
q\in\Theta
such that the simple portion
\mathcal{I}_{q}
is contained in the interior of the 2d-part of
\tau
(indeed, those simple portions cannot be included
in a boundary component of
\mathbf{S}
).

Theorem
9.4
then shows that (
12.14
) is a Friedman–Ramanujan
function, with the same parameters as in Remarks
1.18
. Hence, the parameters

\mathrm{K}(N,\mathbf{S})=2N+2\chi(\mathbf{S})
and
\mathrm{N}(N,\mathbf{S})=n_{\mathbf{S}}(a_{N+1}+1)+3\chi(\mathbf{S})+1

satisfy the claim of Theorem
12.16
. We obtain an estimate of the
Friedman–Ramanujan norm of the function (
12.14
) by:

\displaystyle\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,\chi(\mathbf{S}))\,\tilde{a}_{N}^{2j}\mathfrak{f}(j,\chi(\tau))e^{6R\chi(\mathbf{S})},

where the term
\mathfrak{f}(j,\chi(\tau))
comes from the requirements on the function
\nu

given in Notation
12.12
.

12.5.6.
Third step: estimate of the remainder term

As in the proof of Lemma
12.24
,
using (
12.11
), (
12.12
) and the requirements on the function
\nu
coming from
Notation
12.12
, we see that the remainder term in Theorem
12.16

is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,\chi(\mathbf{S}))\frac{\lVert F(\ell)\,e^{(1+\eta)\ell}\rVert_{\infty}}{g^{N+1}}\left(2^{2j(Q+n_{\mathbf{S}})}+\tilde{a}_{N}^{2j}\right)e^{12R\chi(\mathbf{S})}

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\mathbf{x}>0\end{subarray}}\int_{Y\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{S}|\mathbf{S}_{\partial})}e^{-\eta\ell_{Y}({\mathbf{\zeta}})}\prod_{i=1}^{j}e^{\alpha_{i}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{[0,\kappa]}(\alpha_{i})|\nu(\tau_{Y},\vec{\alpha})|\,\mathrm{d}\vec{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Vol}^{\mathrm{WP}}_{g_{\mathbf{S}},n_{\mathbf{S}}}(\mathbf{x},Y).

As in the proof of Lemma
12.24
, we conclude by integrating
\vec{\alpha}
on

[0,\kappa]^{j}
and bounding the final integral, introducing a multi-loop filling
\tau
.

13.
Proof of the spectral gap result

Theorem
12.16
was the last brick in the proof of Theorem
1.6
, namely
that, given
\alpha<1/2
and arbitrary small
\epsilon>0
,

(13.1)

\displaystyle\lim_{g\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left(\lambda_{1}(X)\leq\frac{1}{4}-\alpha^{2}-\epsilon\right)=0.

The full argument leading to Theorem
1.6
from Theorem
12.16
is given in
our exposition paper
[
4
]
. Here, we outline the main steps.

13.1.
Trace method

The trace method consists in controlling the expectation of a sum over the set
\mathcal{G}(X)
of all
primitive closed geodesics of
X
:

(13.2)

\left\langle F\right\rangle_{g,\kappa,R}=\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)}F(\ell(\gamma))\,\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}}(X)\Bigg]

where we restricted the Weil–Petersson measure to the set
\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}
of all

(\kappa,R)
-tangle-free surfaces, following the definition of
[
12
,
3
]
. The
test function
F=F_{m,L}
will be of the form

(13.3)

\displaystyle F_{m,L}(\ell)=\ell e^{-\ell/2}\mathcal{D}^{m}H_{L}(\ell)

where
H_{L}
is itself a smooth function whose support is
[-L,L]
, constructed by dilation of
the function for
L=1
, i.e.
H_{L}(\ell):=H_{1}(\ell/L)
and
\mathcal{D}
is the differential operator

\frac{1}{4}-\partial^{2}
.

The introduction of the operator
\mathcal{D}
is one of the keys of our approach, as it will give rise to
cancellations in (
13.2
), leading to subexponential growth. The parameters

\kappa
,
R
and
L
will be determined later;
\kappa
is a fixed, arbitrary small number, and

R=\kappa\log g
; similarly,
L
will grow logarithmically with the genus
g
. The important
point is that the tangle-free set
\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}
is a set of large probability (see
[
4
, Lemma 4.4]
),
namely that, for
\kappa<\kappa_{0}=\min(2/3,2\argsh 1)
,

(13.4)

1-\mathbb{P}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left(\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}\right)\leq C_{\mathrm{tf}}(\kappa^{2}+g^{\frac{3}{2}\kappa-1}).

From
[
4
]
, it suffices to show that the expectation (
13.2
) is

o(e^{(\alpha+\epsilon)L})
as
g\rightarrow+\infty
. More precisely, the Selberg trace formula
together with the Markov inequality allows to write

(13.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left(\delta\leq\lambda_{1}\leq\frac{1}{4}-\alpha^{2}-\epsilon\right)\leq\frac{C}{e^{(\alpha+\epsilon)L}}\Big(\left\langle F_{m,L}\right\rangle_{g,\kappa,R}+g\log(g)^{2}\Big)+C_{\mathrm{tf}}(\kappa^{2}+g^{\frac{3}{2}\kappa-1})

for all
\delta>0
(see
[
4
, Lemma 2.7]
). We therefore need to have

e^{(\alpha+\epsilon)L}\geq g^{1+\eta}
for a
\eta>0
, in order to balance the terms growing linearly
with
g
(including, in particular, the topological term from the Selberg trace formula). From now
on, we take
L=A\log g
for an integer
A\geq\alpha+\epsilon
.

13.2.
Restriction to a finite number of types

We now restrict the number of topological configurations in the average
\left\langle F_{m,L}\right\rangle_{g,\kappa,R}
by using
asymptotic expansions in powers of
1/g
and the tangle-free hypothesis. First, letting

{\chi_{+}}=2A+1
, we observe that we can replace the average
\left\langle F_{m,L}\right\rangle_{g,\kappa,R}
by a
truncated version
\left\langle F_{m,L}\right\rangle_{g,\kappa,R,{\chi_{+}}}
, where for a general test function
F
,

\displaystyle\left\langle F\right\rangle_{g,\kappa,R,{\chi_{+}}}:=\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\mathbf{T}:\chi(\mathbf{T})\leq{\chi_{+}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)\\
\gamma\sim\mathbf{T}\end{subarray}}F(\ell(\gamma))\,{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}}(X)\Bigg].

The possibility to restrict the sum to local topological types
\mathbf{T}
of absolute Euler
characteristic
\chi(\mathbf{T})\leq{\chi_{+}}
comes from standard estimates recalled in §
B
(see
(
B.2
)).

We now decompose this average according to the local types it contains, i.e. we write

(13.6)

\displaystyle\left\langle F\right\rangle_{g,\kappa,R,{\chi_{+}}}=\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{{\chi_{+}}}^{\kappa,R,L}}\langle F\rangle_{g,\kappa,R}^{{\mathbf{T}}}

where
\mathrm{Loc}_{{\chi_{+}}}^{\kappa,R,L}
is the set of local topological types of loops,
filling a surface of Euler characteristic bounded by
{\chi_{+}}
, that can be realized by a closed
geodesic of length
\leq L
in a
(\kappa,R)
-tangle-free surface, and for

\mathbf{T}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{{\chi_{+}}}^{\kappa,R,L}

(13.7)

\displaystyle\langle F\rangle_{g,\kappa,R}^{{\mathbf{T}}}:=\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)\\
\gamma\sim\mathbf{T}\end{subarray}}F(\ell(\gamma))\,{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}}(X)\Bigg].

We know from
[
3
]
that for
R=\kappa\log g
and
L=A\log g
,

(13.8)

\#\mathrm{Loc}_{{\chi_{+}}}^{\kappa,R,L}\leq\mathrm{fn}(\kappa,A,{\chi_{+}})(\log g)^{n_{\mathrm{typ}}}

for a constant
n_{\mathrm{typ}}=\mathrm{fn}(\kappa,A,{\chi_{+}})>0
depending only on
\kappa
,
A
and

{\chi_{+}}
.

13.3.
Expression of the indicator function using the Moebius function

Thanks to the result of
[
3
]
, we can express the indicator function
\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}}
in terms of the Moebius function
\mu_{\kappa,R}
: for every hyperbolic surface
X
,

(13.9)

\displaystyle 1-\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}}(X)=\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{S}(X)}\mu_{\kappa,R}(\tau)

where the sum runs over all the geodesic sub-c-surfaces of
X
.
We can then rewrite (
13.6
) as

(13.10)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{{\chi_{+}}}^{\kappa,R,L}}\langle F\rangle_{g}^{{\mathbf{T}}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\\
\chi(\mathbf{S})<{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{T}}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}}\langle\mu_{\kappa,R}\otimes F\rangle_{g,Q}^{{\mathfrak{T}}}

up to a small error term. The set
\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}
now is the set of local topological types
\mathfrak{T}
of
lc-surfaces
(\mathbf{S},\mathbf{\zeta},\tau)
such that:

•

the loop
\mathbf{\zeta}
fills a surface of absolute Euler characteristic
\leq{\chi_{+}}
, and has a
topology that can be realised in a tangle-free surface with a geodesic having length less than

L
;

•

the absolute Euler characteristic of
\tau
is less than
\chi_{+}^{\prime}
;

•

the topology of the pair
(\zeta,\tau)
is constrained by the fact that it can be
realised in a hyperbolic surface
Z
, in which
\ell_{Z}(\zeta)\leq L
and
\tau
is a

(\kappa,R)
-derived tangle.

We recall from (
12.2
) that

(13.11)

\displaystyle\langle\mu_{\kappa,R}\otimes F\rangle_{g,Q}^{{\mathfrak{T}}}:=\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)\\
\tau\in\mathcal{S}_{Q}(X)\\
(\gamma,\tau)\sim{\mathfrak{T}}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\kappa,R}(\tau)F(\ell(\gamma))\Bigg].

Note that the sum
\sum_{\mathbf{S}}
runs over all c-surfaces of absolute Euler characteristic less than
some
{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}
to be chosen later (we assume without loss of generality that

{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}\leq{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}
). The set of such c-surfaces is infinite, because
\mathbf{S}
could contain
an arbitrary number of 1d-components (which do not contribute to the Euler characteristic). Thus
we prefer to rewrite the second sum in (
13.10
) as

(13.12)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\\
\chi(\mathbf{S})<{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}\\
\mathfrak{c}(\mathbf{S})=0\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{T}}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}}\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\langle\mu_{\kappa,R}\otimes F\rangle_{g,Q}^{{\rho_{j}\mathfrak{T}}},

where the first sum, running over purely 2d c-surfaces of bounded Euler characteristic, is now
finite. Recall that the operation
\rho_{j}
consists in adding
j
1d components to a c-surface.

The passage from (
13.6
) to (
13.10
) is detailed in §
B.3
;
the small error term comes from truncating the Euler characteristic of the local topological types,
and from the introduction of the large integer
Q
.

Now, we showed in
[
3
]
that, if
L=A\log g
,
R=\kappa\log g
,
\mathfrak{c}(\mathbf{S})=0

and
\chi(\mathbf{S})<{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}
, then

(13.13)

\#\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}\leq\mathrm{fn}(\kappa,A,{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}})g^{10\chi_{+}^{\prime}{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}\kappa}.

Therefore, we shall take
\kappa
very small so that this polynomial contribution in
g
does not
interfere with our method.

The important thing here is that we have removed the indicator function
\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}}
from the average
we consider. This allows to use Theorems
1.16
and
12.16
that describe
the special structure of the volume functions associated to
\langle F\rangle_{g}^{{\mathbf{T}}}
and

\langle\mu_{\kappa,R}\otimes F\rangle_{g,Q}^{{\mathfrak{T}}}
.

13.4.
Cancellation and the Friedman–Ramanujan hypothesis

Theorems
1.16
and
12.16
can be combined with the integration by parts
phenomenon presented in
[
2
, Proposition 3.18]
to obtain the following propositions. The
underlying principle is that
\mathcal{D}^{m}
vanishes on
\mathbb{C}_{m-1}[\ell]e^{\ell/2}
, hence the choice of
our test function (
13.3
).

First, Theorem
1.16
implies the following, related to the average

\langle F_{m,L}\rangle_{g}^{{\mathbf{T}}}
.

Proposition 13.1

(
[
2
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{T}=[\mathbf{S},\mathbf{c}]_{loc}
be a local topological type with
\chi(\mathbf{S})\leq{\chi_{+}}
. For any
integer
N\geq 0
, there exists constants
c,\mathrm{K}=\mathrm{fn}(N,{\chi_{+}})
such that, for any large
enough
g
, any
m\geq\mathrm{K}
, any
\eta>0
and any
L\geq 1
,

\langle F_{m,L}\rangle_{g}^{{\mathbf{T}}}\leq\mathrm{fn}(N,{\chi_{+}},\eta,m)\Big(L^{c}+\frac{e^{L(\frac{1}{2}+\eta)}}{g^{N+1}}\Big).

Summing over
\mathbf{T}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{{\chi_{+}}}^{\kappa,R,L}
, and using
(
13.8
) to bound the number of terms in the sum, we obtain that for
R=\kappa\log g
and
L=A\log g
,

(13.14)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{{\chi_{+}}}^{\kappa,R,L}}\langle F_{m,L}\rangle_{g}^{{\mathbf{T}}}\leq\mathrm{fn}(N,\kappa,A,{\chi_{+}},m,\eta)L^{n_{\mathrm{typ}}}\Big(L^{c}+\frac{e^{L(\frac{1}{2}+\eta)}}{g^{N+1}}\Big)

where we recall that
c=\mathrm{fn}(N,{\chi_{+}})
and
n_{\mathrm{typ}}=\mathrm{fn}(\kappa,A,{\chi_{+}})
.

We now state a similar property for the average (
13.12
), now using Theorem

12.16
.

Proposition 13.2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{T}}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}
for a filling type
\mathbf{S}
such that
\mathfrak{c}(\mathbf{S})=0
and

\chi(\mathbf{S})\leq{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}
. For any integer
N\geq 0
, there exists constants

c,\mathrm{K}=\mathrm{fn}(N,{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}})
such that, for any large enough
g
, any
m\geq\mathrm{K}
, any

\eta>0
and any
L\geq 1
,
R=\kappa\log(g)
,
U_{3}(\chi):=U_{2}(\chi)+13\chi
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\langle\mu_{\kappa,R}\otimes F_{m,L}\rangle_{g,Q}^{{\rho_{j}{\mathfrak{T}}}}\leq\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}},\kappa,m,\eta)\,g^{\kappa U_{3}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})}\left(L^{c}+\frac{e^{L(\frac{1}{2}+\eta)}}{g^{N+1}}\right).

Proof.

We use our cancellation argument with the Friedman–Ramanujan function

h_{g,Q,N}^{\mu_{\kappa,R},\mathfrak{T},j}
, and sum the contribution associated to each

j
, using the fact that, for the Moebius function
\mu_{\kappa,R}
,
\mathfrak{f}(j,\chi)=U_{1}(\chi)e^{RU_{2}(\chi)}/j!
and hence the constant appearing is smaller than

(13.15)

\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}})e^{R(U_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})+13{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}})}\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\frac{M^{j}}{j!}=\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}})g^{\kappa U_{3}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})+M}

where
M=\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}},\kappa)
.
∎

Summing over all filling types
\mathbf{S}
such that
\mathfrak{c}(\mathbf{S})=0
and

\chi(\mathbf{S})\leq{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}
and all local topological types
{\mathfrak{T}}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}
(the cardinality
of which is bounded by (
13.13
)), we obtain an integer
\mathrm{K}
such that, for any
m\geq\mathrm{K}
,

(13.16)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\\
\chi(\mathbf{S})<{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}\\
\mathfrak{c}(\mathbf{S})=0\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{T}}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}}\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\langle\mu_{\kappa,R}\otimes F_{m,L}\rangle_{g,Q}^{{\rho_{j}{\mathfrak{T}}}}\leq\mathrm{fn}(Q,N,{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}},\kappa,m,\eta)g^{\kappa U_{4}({\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}})}\left(L^{c}+\frac{e^{L(\frac{1}{2}+\eta)}}{g^{N+1}}\right)

where
U_{4}(\chi):=U_{2}(\chi)+13\chi+10\chi^{2}
.
The important thing here is that the bound is subexponential in
L
: there is a polynomial term

L^{c}
, and an exponential term
e^{L(\frac{1}{2}+\eta)}
which is compensated by the factor

g^{-(N+1)}
. Balancing these terms, we can conclude the proof of Theorem
1.6
.

13.5.
Final choice of parameters

Let
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
and
0<\epsilon<\frac{1}{4}-\alpha^{2}
.
We pick our parameters the following way.

•

The order
N
of the expansion is chosen to be an integer so that

2\alpha(N+2)>1
.

•

We take
L=A\log g
with
A=2(N+2)
; this ensures that
e^{L/2}/g^{N+1}=g
, to
match the topological term of the trace formula.

•

{\chi_{+}}=2A+1
to be able to limit the local types of loops filling a surface of Euler
characteristic
<{\chi_{+}}
(as prescribed after (
B.2
)).

•

\chi_{+}^{\prime}=2A+4
and
Q=2A+5+6\chi_{+}^{\prime}
, as recommended in Proposition

B.6
, to reduce the possibilities to derived tangles
\tau
with

\chi(\tau)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}
with a 1d-component separating at most
Q
connected components.

•

{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathrm{fn}(A,{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime})
is prescribed in Proposition
B.9
, implying that
we can restrict attention to c-filling types
\mathbf{S}
with
\chi(\mathbf{S})<{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}
.

•

We can now apply Propositions
13.1
and
13.2

with a parameter
\eta<\epsilon
.

•

This allows to fix the integer
m
in the definition of
F_{m,L}
: it is chosen as the maximum
of the integers given by Propositions
13.1
and

13.2
, for the finite number of c-filling types
\mathbf{S}
with

\chi(\mathbf{S})<{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}
and
\mathfrak{c}(\mathbf{S})=0
.

•

As announced, we take
R=\kappa\log g
, where we now pick the parameter

\kappa
so that

\begin{cases}0<\kappa<\min(1/10,2\argsh 1)\\
(U_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})+36\chi_{+}^{\prime})\kappa<1\\
U_{4}({\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}})\kappa<\alpha A-1\end{cases}

as recommended in Proposition
B.6
and to ensure that the upper bound
obtained in equation (
13.15
) is
\mathcal{O}(e^{(\alpha+\eta)L})
.

Then, for
F_{m,L}(\ell)=\ell\mathcal{D}^{m}H_{L}(\ell)e^{-\ell/2}
, the expected trace
(
13.2
) is
o(e^{(\alpha+\epsilon)L})
as
g\rightarrow+\infty
. By the
inequality (
13.5
), this allows to conclude that

\displaystyle\limsup_{g\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{P}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left(\delta\leq\lambda_{1}\leq\frac{1}{4}-\alpha^{2}-\epsilon\right)\leq C_{\mathrm{tf}}\kappa^{2}

for the universal constant
C_{\mathrm{tf}}
from (
13.4
).
Since
\kappa
can be taken to be arbitrarily small, this concludes the proof of Theorem

1.6
.

Appendix A
Following constants in
[
1
]
and
[
2
]

In this short appendix, we explicit some constants from the literature. It is useful to recall the
notation used in
[
1
]
,

V_{g,n}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}\leq 3g-3+n}c_{g,n}(\alpha)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{x_{i}^{2\alpha_{i}}}{2^{2\alpha_{i}}(2\alpha_{i}+1)!}

for the total Weil–Petersson volume of
\mathcal{M}_{g,n}(\mathbf{x})
. The constant coefficient of this polynomial
is
c_{g,n}(0)=V_{g,n}(0)=V_{g,n}
.

A.1.
Constants in
[
1
]

The main result of
[
1
]
, Theorem 1.1, reads as follows.

Theorem A.1
.

For any integers
g\geq 0
,
n\geq 1
such that
2g-2+n>0
, there exists a family of

n
-variable even polynomial functions
(P_{g,n}^{(N,V_{\pm})})_{N,V_{\pm}}
, with
N\geq 0
and

V_{+}\sqcup V_{-}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}
, such that for any integer
N\geq 0
and any length
vector
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n}
,

(A.1)

\Big|\frac{x_{1}\ldots x_{n}V_{g,n}(\mathbf{x})}{V_{g,n}}-F_{g,n}^{(N)}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\leq\mathrm{fn}(N,n)\frac{(\|\mathbf{x}\|+1)^{3N+1}}{(g+1)^{N+1}}\exp\left(\frac{x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}}{2}\right)

where

F_{g,n}^{(N)}(\mathbf{x}):=\sum_{V_{+}\sqcup V_{-}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}}P_{g,n}^{(N,V_{\pm})}(\mathbf{x})\prod_{i\in V_{+}}\cosh\left(\frac{x_{i}}{2}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{-}}\sinh\left(\frac{x_{i}}{2}\right).

Furthermore, we have the following.

(1)

If
V_{0}:=\{1,\ldots,n\}\setminus(V_{-}\sqcup V_{+})
, then the polynomial

P_{g,n}^{(N,V_{\pm})}
is odd in the variable
(x_{i})_{i\in V_{0}\cup V_{+}}
and even in the
variables
(x_{i})_{i\in V_{-}}
.

(2)

The total degree of
P_{g,n}^{(N,V_{\pm})}
in the variables
(x_{i})_{i\in V_{+}\sqcup V_{-}}
is

\leq 2N
. The partial degree with respect to each
x_{i}
with
i\in V_{0}
can be bounded by
a quantity
a_{N+1}=\mathrm{fn}(N)
.

(3)

The coefficients of
P_{g,n}^{(N,V_{\pm})}
can be written as linear combinations
(independent of
g
) of the
c_{g,n}(\alpha)/V_{g,n}
for multi-indices
\alpha
such that

\sup_{1\leq i\leq n}\alpha_{i}\leq 2N+a_{N+1}
.

(4)

There exists
\tilde{a}_{N}=\mathrm{fn}(N)
such that the constant in (
A.1
) and the
coefficients of the polynomials
P_{g,n}^{(N,V_{\pm})}
are bounded by
\tilde{a}_{N}^{n}
,
uniformly in
g
.

The number of boundary components
n
will be set to be
n=n_{\mathbf{S}}+2j
for the purposes of this paper,
with
n_{\mathbf{S}}
fixed and
j
arbitrary. We saw in §
12
how essential it is to understand
the dependency in
n
(i.e.,
j
) of the constants.

Proof.

The expression, and the first three points, are explicitely addressed in
[
1
]

(we multiplied the result of
[
1
, Theorem 1.1]
by
x_{1}\ldots x_{n}
to make it
directly usable for our purposes).

However, the last point requires to follow the proof of
[
1
, Theorem 1.1]
to
check how all the constants
C_{n}
, present in
[
1
]
, depend on the number
n
of
boundary components (both for the constant in (
A.1
) and the coefficients of the
polynomials).

Thanks to
[
13
, Lemma 24]
, the constants
C_{n}
in
[
1
, eq (20)]
and

[
1
, Lemma 2.4]
may be seen to be uniform in
n
. In
[
1
, Lemma
2.8]
, the constant
C_{n}
may be of order at most
2^{n}
, because the number of
terms in the sum
[
1
, eq (24)]
is
2^{n}
. This leads to the conclusion that the
constant
C_{n,N}
in
[
1
, Theorem 4.2]
can be bounded by
2^{2nN}
.

After establishing
[
1
, Theorem 4.2]
, the proof of Theorem

A.1
is done in §5.4 in
[
1
]
, and consists in
applying
[
1
, Lemma 5.3]
to the function

f(\alpha):=c_{g,n}(\alpha)/V_{g,n}
. It is known that

0\leq c_{g,n}(\alpha)\leq c_{g,n}(0)=V_{g,n},

see e.g.
[
9
, Lemma 3.1]
, so we mainly need to check how
[
1
, Lemma
5.3]
depends on
n
.

When applying
[
1
, Lemma 5.3]
to bound the error term, we must take

|m|=2N+1
,
p=N+1
,
a=a_{N+1}
, so there arises a constant
C_{n,a,p,2N}
bounded
by
2^{N+1+n}a_{N+1}^{n}(4n)^{N+1}n^{2N+1}
. Hence, the error term in
[
1
, Lemma
5.3]
is less than

2^{2nN}2^{N+1+n}a_{N+1}^{n}(4n)^{N+1}n^{2N+1},

giving the desired control for
the constant in (
A.1
).

We now turn to the control on the coefficients of the polynomial
P_{g,n}^{(N,V_{\pm})}
. In

[
1
, Lemma 5.3]
, the “linear combinations of the values

\delta^{\mathbf{m}}f(\alpha)
” may be checked to have coefficients bounded by
n^{N+1}
.
Given that
|\mathbf{m}|\leq 2N
and
|\alpha|\leq a_{N+1}
, the number of possible terms is
at most
(a_{N+1}+1)^{n}(N+1)^{n}
. Finally

|\delta^{\mathbf{m}}f(\alpha)|\leq 2^{2N}\sup_{\alpha}|f(\alpha)|=2^{2N}f(0)
, where
f(0)=1
. The outcome is that each coefficient of
P_{g,n}^{(N,V_{\pm})}

is bounded by

n^{N+1}2^{2N}(a_{N+1}+1)^{n}(N+1)^{n}

which implies our claim.
∎

A.2.
A constant in
[
2
]

For a realisation
\mathfrak{R}
of
\rho_{j}\mathbf{S}
into a surface of genus
g
, the notion of “rank”

r_{g}^{\rho_{j}\mathbf{S}}(\mathfrak{R})
is defined in
[
2
, §5.4.1]
. The idea is that the rank of
a realization is the height in the asymptotic expansion in powers of
1/g
at which it appears. This
allows us to truncate the sum over all realizations to those of rank
<N
, as done in

[
2
, Lemma 5.20]
. This result can be adapted to our needs, in the following form.

Lemma A.2
.

There exists a universal constant
D>0
such that, for any c-filling type
\mathbf{S}
with

\mathfrak{c}(\mathbf{S})=0
, any integers
N\geq\chi(\mathbf{S})
,
Q\geq 0
, any large enough
g
, any

0\leq j\leq\log(g)
,

(A.2)

\frac{V_{g_{\mathbf{S}},n_{\mathbf{S}}}}{V_{g}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{R}\in R_{g,Q}(\rho_{j}\mathbf{S})\\
r_{g}^{\rho_{j}\mathbf{S}}(\mathfrak{R})\geq N\end{subarray}}V_{\mathfrak{R}}(\mathbf{0})\leq 2^{(n_{\mathbf{S}}+2j)(Q+n_{\mathbf{S}})}\frac{D^{Q+n_{\mathbf{S}}}}{g^{N}}.

Remark A.3
.

The rank of a realisation is always greater than
\chi(\mathbf{S})
, and the argument leading to
(
A.2
) also shows that for any realisation

\mathfrak{R}\in R_{g,Q}(\rho_{j}\mathbf{S})
, we have for each individual term

(A.3)

\frac{V_{g_{\mathbf{S}},n_{\mathbf{S}}}}{V_{g}}V_{\mathfrak{R}}(\mathbf{0})\leq\frac{D^{Q+n_{\mathbf{S}}}}{g^{\chi(\mathbf{S})}}.

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of
[
2
, Lemma 5.20]
, but one needs to follow the
dependency on
j
of the implied constants. In the first lines of proof, there is an implied
constant
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{S}}\left(1\right)
which is the number of partitions of
\partial\mathbf{S}
. In the current
context, this is replaced by the number of partitions of
\tilde{\partial}\mathbf{S}
into at most
Q+n_{\mathbf{S}}

sets. This number is bounded by
2^{(n_{\mathbf{S}}+2j)(Q+n_{\mathbf{S}})}
. A second constant
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{S}}\left(1\right)
counts the
number of subsets of
\{1,\ldots,\mathfrak{q}\}
with
\mathfrak{q}=Q+n_{\mathbf{S}}
, which is less than

2^{Q+n_{\mathbf{S}}}
.

Using
[
13
, Lemma 24]
, we can prove that the implied constant in
[
2
, eq (5.10)]

may be bounded by
CD^{k-1}
for some
D>0
, where
k
is the number of connected components of

S_{g}\setminus\rho_{j}\mathbf{S}
. Since we are only counting realisations such that
k\leq Q+n_{\mathbf{S}}
, the
implied constant in
[
2
, eq (5.10)]
and the following lines is at most
D^{Q+n_{\mathbf{S}}}
.

In
[
2
, eq (5.11)]
,
\mathfrak{q}
now varies from
2
to
Q+n_{\mathbf{S}}
and
n_{j^{+}}
varies from

1
to
n_{\mathbf{S}}+2j-1
. In
[
2
, eq (5.12)]
, we thus need to understand the dependency on
n
of
the bound
\mathcal{O}_{N,n}\left(.\right)
, with
n\leq n_{\mathbf{S}}+2j-1
. Using
[
11
, Theorem 1.8]
, we can
see that this upper bound is uniform as long as
n^{2}=o(g)
, more than we need to treat

n\leq n_{\mathbf{S}}+2j-1
with
n_{\mathbf{S}}
fixed and
j\leq\log g
.
∎

Appendix B
Discarding sets of extremely small probability

In this section, we explain how to discard sets of extremely small probability in the moduli space,
such as surfaces containing strict subsurfaces of large Euler characteristic with short boundaries,
or multi-curves of small lengths disconnecting the surface in many connected components.

By “extremely small probability”, we mean probability
\mathcal{O}(g^{-N})
where we can tune the
parameters to make the exponent
N
as large as we need. This stands in contrast to the set of
surfaces containing tangles, which has probability of order
\kappa^{2}+g^{\frac{3}{2}\kappa-1}
.

The reason why sets of “extremely small probability” can easily be discarded in the trace method
is that they contribute a negligible amount to our trace averages. This comes from the following
deterministic counting estimate for periodic geodesics, adapted from
[
5
, Theorem 4.1.6 and Lemma
6.6.4]
.

Lemma B.1

(
[
2
, Lemma 2.1]
)
.

Let
X
be a hyperbolic surface, compact or with geodesic boundary. For any
L>0
,

\#\{\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)\,:\,\ell(\gamma)\leq L\}\leq 205\,{\chi(X)}\,e^{L}

where
\chi(X)
is the absolute Euler characteristic of
X
. As a consequence, if
F
is a
test function supported in
[-L,L]
, then

(B.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)}|F(\ell(\gamma))|\leq 560\,{\chi(X)}L\lVert F(\ell)e^{\ell}\rVert_{\infty}.

In what follows,
F
is always assumed to be supported in
[-L,L]
.

B.1.
Surfaces of large (but fixed) Euler characteristic and short boundary

The following proposition is useful to give a probabilistic bound on the Euler characteristic of
the surface filled by a closed geodesic
\gamma
on a surface of large genus
g
.

Proposition B.2
.

The probability for a random hyperbolic
surface of genus
g
to contain a multi-loop of length
\leq L
filling a surface of absolute
Euler characteristic
>\chi
is
\mathcal{O}_{\chi}\left(L^{c(\chi)}e^{L}/g^{\chi+1}\right)
.

Proof.

If there is a multi-loop of length
\leq L
filling a surface of absolute Euler characteristic
greater than
\chi
, then there exists a subsurface of absolute Euler characteristic
\chi+1

and boundary length
\leq 2L
, by
[
3
, Lemma 4.13]
. Then repeat the proof of

[
2
, Proposition 6.7]
.
∎

Together with Lemma
B.1
, this allows to decompose the average

\langle F\rangle_{g}^{{\text{all}}}
into

(B.2)

\displaystyle\langle F\rangle_{g}^{{\text{all}}}=\sum_{\mathbf{T}:\chi(\mathbf{T})\leq{\chi_{+}}}\langle F\rangle_{g}^{{\mathbf{T}}}+\mathcal{O}_{\chi_{+}}\Big(\frac{L^{c({\chi_{+}})+1}e^{2L}}{g^{{\chi_{+}}}}\lVert F\rVert_{\infty}\Big)

if
F
is supported in
[-L,L]
(note that here we used the fact that the Euler characteristic of a
surface of genus
g
is linear in
g
). If
L=A\log g
for an integer
A
, and we specify

{\chi_{+}}=2A+1
, then the error term is
\mathcal{O}_{A}(\lVert F\rVert_{\infty})
. As a consequence, for our
prospects, it is enough to study the term
\sum_{\chi(\mathbf{T})\leq{\chi_{+}}}\langle F\rangle_{g}^{{\mathbf{T}}}
for
arbitrarily large fixed
{\chi_{+}}
.

B.2.
Short multi-curves separating into at least
Q
connected components.

Recall that, for an integer
Q
, we denote as
{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)
the set of multi-curves which separate the
surface
X
into at most
Q
connected components. We shall estimate the probability of the
following events.

Notation B.3
.

For an integer
Q\geq 1
,
g\geq 2
and
0<\kappa<1
, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}:=\left\{X\in\mathcal{M}_{g}\,:\,\forall c\text{ multi-curve on }X,\ell^{\mathrm{max}}(c)\leq\kappa\Rightarrow\,c\in\mathrm{MC}_{X}(Q)\right\}

where
\ell^{\mathrm{max}}
denotes the length of the longest component of the multi-curve
c
.

We shall prove the following bound on the probability of the event
\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}
.

Proposition B.4
.

For any
Q\geq 1
,
g\geq 2
,
0<\kappa<1
,

(B.3)

\displaystyle 1-\mathbb{P}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}\right)\leq\frac{\mathrm{fn}(\kappa,Q)}{g^{Q-1}}.

In other words, the events
\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}
are events of extremely small probability: we can
adjust the parameter
Q
to match any desired rate of decay.

In order to prove this bound, let us introduce a counting function
\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q}
, defined for a
compact hyperbolic surface
X
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q}(X)=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)\\
\mathfrak{c}(c)=j\end{subarray}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{[0,\kappa]}(\ell_{X}^{\mathrm{max}}(c))

where we recall that
\mathfrak{c}(c)
is the number of components of the multi-curve
c
. This
function counts the total number of unordered multi-curves that disconnect
X
into at most
Q

components, and with maximal length
\leq\kappa
. More generally, for any
\beta>0
, we define a weighted version

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q,\beta}(X)=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\beta^{j}}{j!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)\\
\mathfrak{c}(c)=j\end{subarray}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{[0,\kappa]}(\ell_{X}^{\mathrm{max}}(c)).

The sum actually stops at
j=3g-3
, but we want to estimate the expectation of
\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q,\beta}

uniformly as
g
varies. We prove the following.

Proposition B.5
.

For any fixed
\beta>0
,
Q\geq 1
and
n
,

\displaystyle\sup_{g}\mathbb{E}_{g,n}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left[\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q,\beta}\right]<+\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{g}\mathbb{E}_{g,n}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left[\mathcal{Y}^{2}_{\kappa,Q,\beta}\right]<+\infty.

Proof.

We use the upper bound

V_{g_{i},n_{i}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n_{i}})\leq V_{g_{i},n_{i}}\exp\left(\frac{x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n_{i}}}{2}\right),

for any
g_{i},n_{i}
which comes from the bound of
[
9
, Lemma 3.2]
on the
coefficients of volume polynomials. By standard integration methods on the moduli space, this
gives

(B.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g,n}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left[\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q,\beta}\right]\leq\frac{1}{V_{g,n}}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\beta^{j}I_{\kappa}^{j}}{j!}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{Q}\sum_{\vec{g},\vec{n}}\#\mathrm{Orb}_{g,n}^{j,Q}(\vec{g},\vec{n})\prod_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{q}}V_{g_{i},n_{i}}

where:

•

the sum over
1\leq\mathfrak{q}\leq Q
is a sum over the number of connected components of
the complement of
c
;

•

the sum over
\vec{g},\vec{n}
is a sum over the vectors

(g_{i},n_{i})_{1\leq i\leq\mathfrak{q}}
satisfying
2g_{i}-2+n_{i}>0
,

\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{q}}n_{i}=2j\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{q}}(2g_{i}-2+n_{i})=2g-2+n;

•

the quantity
I_{\kappa}
is defined as
I_{\kappa}=\int_{0}^{\kappa}\ell e^{\frac{\ell}{2}}d\ell
;

•

\mathrm{Orb}_{g,n}^{j,Q}(\vec{g},\vec{n})
is the set of MCG-equivalence classes, in a
surface
S_{g,n}
of signature
(g,n)
, of multi-curves
c=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{j})
with
j

components, cutting the surface
S_{g,n}
into
\mathfrak{q}
numbered pieces of respective signatures

(g_{1},n_{1}),\ldots,(g_{\mathfrak{q}},n_{\mathfrak{q}})
.

We then prove that for each
\vec{g},\vec{n}
,

\#\mathrm{Orb}_{g,n}^{j,Q}(\vec{g},\vec{n})\leq\mathfrak{q}^{2j}.

To see this, we define a surjective map from a subset

\mathcal{A}\subset\{1,\ldots,\mathfrak{q}\}^{2j}
onto

\mathrm{Orb}_{g,n}^{j,Q}(\vec{g},\vec{n})
. Fix a family of surfaces

(S_{i})_{1\leq i\leq\mathfrak{q}}
of respective signatures

(g_{i},n_{i})_{1\leq i\leq\mathfrak{q}}
. Given a sequence
(i_{k},i_{k}^{\prime})_{1\leq k\leq j}
with

(i_{k},i_{k}^{\prime})\in\{1,\ldots,\mathfrak{q}\}^{2}
, glue successively, for
k=1,\ldots,j
, a
boundary curve of
S_{i_{k}}
to a boundary curve of
S_{i_{k}^{\prime}}
, and call this curve
c_{k}
. The set

\mathcal{A}
is the subset of
\{1,\ldots,\mathfrak{q}\}^{2j}
such that this succession of gluings is
actually possible and gives a connected surface of signature
(g,n)
. In this case, we obtain a
surface of signature
(g,n)
together with a muticurve
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{j})
, that can be mapped to
our reference surface
S_{g,n}
by a homeomorphism. Any multi-curve that cuts
S_{g,n}
into

\mathfrak{q}
pieces of respective signatures

(g_{1},n_{1}),\ldots,(g_{\mathfrak{q}},n_{\mathfrak{q}})
is MCG-equivalent to a multi-curve obtained
this way.

Now, for fixed
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathfrak{q}})
, we know from
[
13
, Lemma 24]
that

(B.5)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1},\ldots,g_{\mathfrak{q}},\\
\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{q}}(2g_{i}-2+n_{i})=2g-2+n\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{q}}V_{g_{i},n_{i}}\leq C\Big(\frac{D}{2g-2+n}\Big)^{{\mathfrak{q}}-1}V_{g,n}

for universal constants
C,D
.
In particular, this is bounded above by
CV_{g,n}
.
Taking into account all the possibilities for
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathfrak{q}})
, we lose another
factor
(2j)^{Q}
. We end up with the upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g,n}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left[\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q,\beta}\right]\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\beta^{j}}{j!}Q^{2j+1}(2j)^{Q}I_{\kappa}^{j}

which is a convergent series.

The bound on the second moment comes from the inequality

\mathcal{Y}^{2}_{\kappa,Q,\beta}\leq\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q,4\beta}
, together with the previous case.
∎

We now conclude to the proof of Proposition
B.4
, by the same method.

Proof of Proposition
B.4
.

For a hyperbolic surface
X
, define
{\mathrm{MC}}^{(1)}_{X}(Q)\subset{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)
to be the set of
multi-curves which separate
X
into
exactly

Q
components, and
\mathcal{Y}^{(1)}_{\kappa,Q}

the random variable

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{(1)}_{\kappa,Q}(X)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in{\mathrm{MC}}^{(1)}_{X}(Q)\\
\mathfrak{c}(c)=j\end{subarray}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{[0,\kappa]}(\ell_{X}^{\mathrm{max}}(c))

which counts the total number of such multi-curves, with maximal length
\leq\kappa
.

Using the bound (
B.5
) with
\mathfrak{q}=Q
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left[\mathcal{Y}^{(1)}_{\kappa,Q}\right]\leq\frac{D^{Q}}{g^{Q-1}}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{Q^{2j}(2j)^{Q}I_{\kappa}^{j}}{j!}=:\frac{\mathrm{fn}(\kappa,Q)}{g^{Q-1}}.

This yields the announced results, because
\mathcal{Y}^{(1)}_{\kappa,Q}\geq 1
on the complement of
\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}
.

∎

B.3.
Application of the Moebius inversion formula and choice of the integer
Q

We are now ready to we control the cost of going from (
13.6
) to (
13.10
),
i.e. check that this truncation of the set of realizations induces a small error that won’t
interfere with the trace method. This is key to restricting the set of realisations in
(
13.10
) and (
13.11
), hence making it possible to apply the results of §
12
. We prove the following.

Proposition B.6
.

Let
\mathbf{T}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{{\chi_{+}}}^{\kappa,R,L}
,
for
L=A\log g
,
R=\kappa\log g
,
\chi_{+}^{\prime}=2A+4
,
Q=2A+5+6\chi_{+}^{\prime}
. Provided that

\kappa(U_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})+37\chi_{+}^{\prime})<1
, with
U_{2}
the sequence in (
12.5
), we
have:

(B.6)

\displaystyle\langle F\rangle_{g,\kappa,R}^{{\mathbf{T}}}=\left\langle F\right\rangle_{g}^{{\mathbf{T}}}-\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(c,\sigma)\in\mathcal{S}(X)\\
\chi(\sigma)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\\
c\in\,{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)\\
\gamma\sim\mathbf{T}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\kappa,R}(c,\sigma)F(\ell(\gamma))\Bigg]+\mathcal{O}_{A}\left(\|F\|_{\infty}\right).

Proof.

By definition, we have that
\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}\subset\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}
for any
Q
. Furthermore, if

\tau_{\kappa,R}(X)
denotes the maximal
(\kappa,R)
-derived tangle of
X
, defined in

[
3
]
, we have
\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}\subset\{\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\}
because, on

\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}
, the property “all derived tangles have absolute Euler characteristic less than
\chi_{+}^{\prime}
” is
trivially true. We can rewrite these inclusions in the form

\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}}=\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{TF}_{g}^{\kappa,R}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}}\mathds{1}_{[0,\chi_{+}^{\prime})}(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X)).

The Moebius inversion formula from
[
3
]
allows to rewrite the error term in
(
B.6
) as
E_{1}+E_{2}
, where

(B.7)

E_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\mathds{1}_{[\chi_{+}^{\prime},+\infty)}(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X)))\Bigg(1-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(c,\sigma)\in\mathcal{S}(X)\\
\chi(\sigma)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\\
c\in\,{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)\end{subarray}}\mu_{\kappa,R}(c,\sigma)\Bigg)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)\\
\gamma\sim\mathbf{T}\end{subarray}}F(\ell(\gamma))\Bigg]

and

(B.8)

E_{2}=\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}}(X)-1)\mathds{1}_{[0,\chi_{+}^{\prime})}(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X)))\Bigg(1-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(c,\sigma)\in\mathcal{S}(X)\\
\chi(\sigma)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\\
c\in\,{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)\end{subarray}}\mu_{\kappa,R}(c,\sigma)\Bigg)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{G}(X)\\
\gamma\sim\mathbf{T}\end{subarray}}F(\ell(\gamma))\Bigg].

We shall bound these two terms separately, using the bound (
12.5
) on the Moebius
function, Lemma
B.1
, as well as our probabilistic bounds on the
events
\{\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))\geq\chi_{+}^{\prime}\}
and
\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}
. This is done
in the two following lemmas, which then allow to conclude.
∎

Lemma B.7
.

We have

|E_{1}|\leq\mathrm{fn}(Q,\chi_{+}^{\prime},\kappa)\|F\|_{\infty}\frac{Le^{L+R(36\chi_{+}^{\prime}+U_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime}))}}{g^{\chi_{+}^{\prime}/2-1}}

and, in particular, if we specify
L=A\log g
,
R=\kappa\log g
,
\chi_{+}^{\prime}=2A+4
and fix a value
of
\kappa
such that
\kappa<(U_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})+36\chi_{+}^{\prime})^{-1}
, then

|E_{1}|\leq\mathrm{fn}(Q,A)\|F\|_{\infty}
.

Proof.

We use the triangle inequality and the deterministic bound from Lemma

B.1
to obtain

(B.9)

|E_{1}|\leq 10^{4}Lge^{L}\|F\|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\mathds{1}_{[\chi_{+}^{\prime},+\infty)}(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X)))\Bigg(1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(c,\sigma)\in\mathcal{S}(X)\\
\chi(\sigma)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\\
c\in\,{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)\end{subarray}}|\mu_{\kappa,R}(c,\sigma)|\Bigg)\Bigg].

Let us now bound the contribution of the Moebius function. We first use the fact that, if

(c,\sigma)
with
c=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{j})
, by
[
3
]
,

|\mu_{\kappa,R}(c,\sigma)|=|\mu_{\kappa,R}(c)||\mu_{\kappa,R}(\sigma)|=\frac{1}{2^{j}j!}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{[0,\kappa]}(\ell_{X}^{\mathrm{max}}(c))|\mu_{\kappa,R}(\sigma)|

which yields

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(c,\sigma)\in\mathcal{S}(X)\\
\chi(\sigma)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\\
c\in\,{\mathrm{MC}}_{X}(Q)\end{subarray}}|\mu(c,\sigma)|\leq(1+\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q})\Big(1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\mathcal{S}(X)\\
\chi(\sigma)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\\
\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)=0\end{subarray}}|\mu(\sigma)|\Big).

This allows us to reduce ourselves to bounding the sum of the Moebius function for purely 2d
c-surfaces. This is done in
[
3
]
, and we obtain a bound of the form

(B.10)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\mathcal{S}(X)\\
\chi(\sigma)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\\
\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)=0\end{subarray}}|\mu(\sigma)|\leq\mathrm{fn}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})g^{3\chi_{+}^{\prime}}e^{RU_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})}.

The factor
g^{3\chi_{+}^{\prime}}
in (
B.10
) is inconvenient. In order to compensate it,
we split the event
\{\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))\geq\chi_{+}^{\prime}\}
depending on the relative position
of
\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R})
and
8\chi_{+}^{\prime}
:

\mathds{1}_{[\chi_{+}^{\prime},+\infty)}(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X)))=\mathds{1}_{[\chi_{+}^{\prime},8\chi_{+}^{\prime})}(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X)))+\mathds{1}_{[8\chi_{+}^{\prime},+\infty)}(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))).

This is helpful because, now, we can improve (
B.10
) on the set where

\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))<8\chi_{+}^{\prime}
:

{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.0mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-4.5mu{\rm{l}}}{1\mskip-5.0mu{\rm{l}}}}_{[\chi_{+}^{\prime},8\chi_{+}^{\prime})}(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X)))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\mathcal{S}(X)\\
\chi(\sigma)<\chi_{+}^{\prime}\\
\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)=0\end{subarray}}|\mu(\sigma)|\leq\mathrm{fn}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})e^{RU_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})}.

Altogether, we obtain

\displaystyle|E_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{fn}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})\|F\|_{\infty}L\,g\,e^{L+RU_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[(1+\mathcal{Y}_{\kappa,Q})\Big(\mathds{1}_{[\chi_{+}^{\prime},+\infty)}(\chi\big(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))\big)+g^{3\chi_{+}^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{[8\chi_{+}^{\prime},+\infty)}(\chi\big(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))\big)\Big)\Bigg].

We then use the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to bound
the expectation above by

(1+\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left[\mathcal{Y}^{2}_{\kappa,Q}\right]^{1/2})\,\Big(\mathbb{P}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))\geq\chi_{+}^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}+g^{3\chi_{+}^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\left(\chi(\tau_{\kappa,R}(X))\geq 8\chi_{+}^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}\Big)

which, Proposition
B.2
and
B.5
, is bounded by

\mathrm{fn}(Q,\chi_{+}^{\prime},\kappa)R^{c(\chi_{+}^{\prime})}\Bigg(\frac{e^{9R\chi_{+}^{\prime}/2}}{g^{\chi_{+}^{\prime}/2}}+g^{3\chi_{+}^{\prime}}\frac{e^{36R\chi_{+}^{\prime}}}{g^{4\chi_{+}^{\prime}}}\Bigg)\leq\mathrm{fn}(Q,\chi_{+}^{\prime},\kappa)\frac{e^{36R\chi_{+}^{\prime}}}{g^{\chi_{+}^{\prime}/2}}

which is our claim.
∎

With exactly the same proof, now using Proposition
B.4
to bound the probability of the
complement of
\mathcal{B}_{g}^{\kappa,Q}
, we obtain:

Lemma B.8
.

We have:

\displaystyle|E_{2}|\leq\mathrm{fn}(Q,\chi_{+}^{\prime})\lVert F\rVert_{\infty}\frac{Le^{L+RU_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})}}{g^{(Q-1)/2-1-3\chi_{+}^{\prime}}}.

In particular, if we specify
L=A\log g
,
R=\kappa\log g
,
Q=2A+5+6\chi_{+}^{\prime}
and fix
\kappa
such
that
\kappa<U_{2}(\chi_{+}^{\prime})^{-1}
, then
|E_{2}|\leq\mathrm{fn}(A,\chi_{+}^{\prime})\|F\|_{\infty}
.

The following proposition shows that we can further restrict the Euler characteristic of
\mathbf{S}
, the
surface filled by
(\mathbf{\zeta},\tau)
:

Proposition B.9
.

If
L=A\log g
and
R=\kappa\log g
, we may find

{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathrm{fn}(A,\chi_{+}^{\prime})
such that, for all
\kappa<1/10
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{S}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{T}}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}}\langle\mu_{\kappa,R}\otimes F\rangle_{g,Q}^{{\mathfrak{T}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\\
\chi(\mathbf{S})<{\chi_{+}^{\prime\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathbf{T}}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbf{S},{\chi_{+}},\chi_{+}^{\prime}}^{\kappa,R,L}}\langle\mu_{\kappa,R}\otimes F\rangle_{g,Q}^{{\mathfrak{T}}}+\mathcal{O}_{A,Q}(1).

We omit the proof, a medley of all the previous arguments.
